# Bugs in vanilla Black & White

openblack replicates vanilla Black & White exactly, bugs included, unless the user decides otherwise. This file lists
every bug or quirk found in vanilla (version 1.20, the BW1W120 executable), with its evidence, what it does in play, and
what openblack does about it.

- **Kept** means openblack does what vanilla does. Nothing goes in `deviations.md`.
- **Fixed** means openblack behaves differently on purpose. That is a deviation: the row here links its row in
  `deviations.md`, where the user's sign-off is recorded.
- **Guarded** means vanilla would crash or read invalid memory. openblack logs vanilla's message (if it has one) and
  carries on without the bad read. Nothing visible changes in play, so it is not a deviation.
- **Open** means it is recorded but nothing is decided or built yet.

Rows are only appended or edited in place, never renumbered or deleted. The `openblack-vanilla-bugs` skill describes
how to find, record and decide them.

| # | Area | The bug in vanilla | Evidence | Effect in play | openblack | Decided | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Buildings (crèche) | A crèche fits its model to the land only in its own create function. A load (also reached by leaving the built temple, which restarts and loads the autosave) rebuilds buildings through their creation virtual, where every other land-following building refits but the crèche doesn't, so a loaded crèche stands rigid at its origin's land height | `Creche::Create` 0x50AB2A fills the lift; `Object::ResolveLoad` 0x63A02E calls the creation virtual only; running 1.20 game after Continue Game: the crèche's 490 lifts all +0.0, every other building filled | Cosmetic: Land 1's Norse crèche fence floats ~0.85–1.2 m on the downhill side and is buried up to 0.8 m on the east | Fixed: always fitted | Deviation #34, user 2026-10-12 | bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/building/creche_terrain_fit.md` |
| 2 | Disciples (trader) | An empty trader asks its own storage pit for the resource it already carries, so it never fetches anything | See deviation #30 | A trader disciple only delivers what it already had | Fixed: an empty trader fetches what the trade town wants | Deviation #30, user 2026-10-12 | `disciple_work::TradeFetchResource` |
| 3 | Graveyard stand-in | When a civic building stands in for a lost graveyard, a death runs the graveyard's non-virtual burial code on it, which counts on the building's own first field (+0xc4) and sets its model status | 0x595e50; class layouts compared | A workshop or totem stand-in shows its scaffold over its finished model; an idle workshop would finish a free scaffold; a dispenser's next miracle comes a turn sooner; a pit or field would hold a bad pointer (crash risk, not reproduced) | Kept (the pointer cases are guarded: openblack counts nothing on them) | User 2026-10-12: "keep vanilla's graveyard quirk but document with evidence" | bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/villager/graveyard_skeletons.md` ("Stand-in burial quirk") |
| 4 | Text drawing | While measuring a line past a carriage return or line feed, the text drawer keeps reading past the break, even past the string's end into whatever memory follows | `GatheringText::DrawText` (see the note) | None visible | Guarded | — | bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/advisors/dyk_bubble.md` |

| 8 | Land detail by CPU | The CPU check counts any family-15 CPU with SSE2 as a "Pentium 4", which takes in every AMD chip from the Athlon 64 to today's Ryzen; modern Intel chips report family 6. That path draws the land in full detail far further out (1600/2500 m instead of 300/600 m) | The CPU check (see the note) | How far fine land reaches depends on the player's CPU maker | Fixed: not based on the CPU; the best lines at Maximum detail, degrading through the lower levels, set in debug | Deviation #35, user 2026-10-12 | bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/rendering/lod_audit.md` |
| 9 | Builders (unreachable place) | A builder whose place can't be reached keeps walking at it for good: arriving farther than 0.2 from its place it walks there again, the walk has no timeout or give-up, and the wall hug has no case for a goal inside an obstacle circle. A worship site's places that miss its model's outline fall back to the site's translation (its temple's middle) + 1 m, inside the temple's 15.05 m walker circle | `ArrivesAtBuildingSite` 0x758AF0, `Living::MoveToPos` 0x5EC270, `MobileWallHug::MoveTo` (bw1-decomp); in openblack, which matches these, Island Wars player 3's worship site sat at 0 % for ~15,000 turns with 14 builders circling the temple | Builders coming from the side a worship site's model leaves open circle the temple until their day ends; the site waits until later builders draw reachable places | Kept | Open (suspected; not confirmed in a vanilla capture) | bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/temple/construction.md` ("Builders with an unreachable place") |
| 10 | Scroll beams | A started gold (or plain) scroll's yellow beam takes its strength from the beat (0.4 to 1) turned to a whole number before it is scaled by 80, not after, so it is 0 except when the beat is exactly 1: only on a frame at the very start of a turn after a turn whose beat level was exactly 1 (about once every 142 s of turns). The intended pulsing yellow beam never shows | The scroll's draw: whole part of the blended beat, then a byte multiply by 0x50, over 0xFFFF00 (bw1-decomp note); simulation of the beat in float | Gold scrolls show their sparks and glow but no beam; silver scrolls keep their faint steady pale blue beam | Fixed: the beat is scaled before it is cut, so the yellow beam beats from alpha 32 to 80 | Deviation #42 (user, 2026-10-12) | `script_highlights::BeamColour`, `Renderer::DrawScrollBeams`; bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/story/scroll_beams.md` |
| 11 | Creature (slapping another's creature) | Every third hard slap in a row at one height sends a bruise packet; its handler takes 0.01 of life from the slapping PLAYER's own creature and adds the bruise to that creature's skin at the place hit on the slapped creature's mesh, so slapping an allied god's creature hurts and marks the player's own creature, at a meaningless place on its skin | The bruise packet's handler (BW1W120 0x63C98D) reads the player's creature (player +0xA4C) for both the life loss and the mark list; the packet carries only the mesh hit record and the strength | The player's own creature loses a hundredth of its life and gets a stray bruise when the player slaps an ally's creature hard (only reachable in multiplayer with alliances, or where another god's creature may be held) | Open: the life loss is kept; the stray mark is not drawn (only a bruise on the slapped creature when it is the player's own) | — | `CreatureHandSystem::Bruise`; bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/creature/creature_touch.md` |
| 12 | Villagers (firemen with nothing remembered) | A fireman whose remembered state is empty goes back into state 0 (invalid), which resumes in itself and has no state, check or exit function, so it stands still for good, still listed among its blaze's firemen. Reached when the object behind its fire reaction (the blaze's first fire) is deleted while it beats another fire of the blaze: the reaction check pops it into beating with nothing left remembered, then pops it again | `Villager::ReactionValidate` 0x756A00 pops when the reacted-to thing is unavailable (`GameThing::ToBeDeleted` 0x56FB70 sets that); `Villager::PopFromPrevious` 0x751E50 resumes in the remembered row's resume state and clears it; info.dat rows 0 (Invalid) and 215 (ReactToFire) resume in 0; `Living::InvalidState` 0x5EC1D0 returns 0 and row 0 has no check; `Villager::StopFireFighting` 0x75B020 resumes the same way with no guard | A fireman stops dead beside where the house was and does nothing until another reaction or script takes it | Fixed: it decides what to do | Deviation #39, user 2026-10-12 ("Fix it (deviation)") | `villager_fire::StateToGoBackTo`; bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/fire/fire_spread_fighting.md` |

| 16 | Fields (crop ripening) | When a field's crop ripens, its town was meant to look again at what it wants (as it does when a field is emptied), but the test asks "is it ripe?" twice, both after the crop has aged, so "not ripe before, ripe now" can never be true and the town is never told | The field's turn of growth (bw1-decomp `Field::Process`, the two ripeness checks around the food update) | A farmer disciple idling with nothing to do isn't sent back to a field the moment it ripens; it finds it on its next look | Kept | — | `src/ECS/Systems/Implementations/FieldSystem.cpp`; bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/villager/farmers_housewives.md` |
| 17 | Creature (names) | A creature's name has room for 63 letters, but the player's creature is given its profile's creature name, which may be 255 letters, by an unbounded copy, as it is made, when a script makes it the player's (CREATURE_SET_PLAYER) and when the profile is chosen or written back. A longer name would run over what follows the name in the creature. | Creature::Initialise 0x474350 (wcscpy into Creature+0xE0, `char16_t name[0x40]`), GScript::CreatureSetPlayer 0x6F429D, PlayerProfile::SetCurrentProfile 0x66B8E1, WriteBackToRegistry 0x66BEF2; the profile's field is `wchar_t CreatureName[256]`. | None in play: the game's own boxes take at most 29 letters, so only a name edited into the registry by hand is long enough. | Guarded: the name has no fixed length, so a long one is kept whole. | 18 | Fields (crop drawing) | A crop sunk past 80% of its height was meant to fade (drawn see-through, from fully opaque at 80% down to 60% opaque when empty), but the fade is written into the crop's colour before the land's light is applied, and applying the light overwrites the whole colour, so the fade never shows. The see-through switch it turns on is not turned off by the draw, but the colour's alpha comes from the tint, which is opaque (the start-up code sets the half-grown and ripe colours' alpha to 0xFF), so it draws as opaque | The field's draw (bw1-decomp `Field::Draw`, 0x528570: the global-alpha byte is set at 0x5287A9, then the colour call at 0x52886A/0x52888A relights the colour with full alpha and multiplies in the tint) | None: a nearly empty crop is simply drawn opaque, 80% under the ground | Kept (no fade drawn) | — | `src/3D/FieldCrop.cpp` (`Sink`); bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/villager/field_crop_drawing.md` |

| 24 | Villagers (firemen already listed) | A villager going into a fire-fighting state from any other state is refused when it is already on its blaze's firemen list, but the refusal leaves it on the list: it goes off deciding what to do and stays counted among the firemen (a "ghost") until it next leaves a fire-fighting state or the blaze ends. The list is moved whole to the next fire when the blaze's first fire cools, so the ghost moves with it. Whether vanilla ever reaches this is unproven: the usual ways a fireman leaves its fire state (exits of both the walk and the fire state it heads for, the reaction's end going back through the fire states) take it off the list first | `Villager::EnterPutOutFire` 0x75ADC0 returns 0 for a listed villager without `RemoveFireman`; `Villager::SetTopState` 0x752010 then enters DecideWhatToDo with no exits; `fn_0072FD20` (root hand-over, from 0x72F721 and `RemoveFromFireGroup` 0x72FDD0) appends the old first fire's list to the new one's; `ReactToFire` 0x765870 counts the list (+0x4C) when weighing whether to fight | If reached, fewer firemen come to that blaze than it needs, one fewer per ghost | Kept | — | `villager_fire::EnterPutOutFire`, `FireSystem::HandOverBlaze`; bw1-decomp `docs/openblack/fire/fire_ghost_firemen.md` |

| 30 | Pathfinding (trying both ways round) | To try a walker's walk both ways round, the game makes a real villager as a stand-in and deletes it after: making it draws the game's random numbers, counts it as a thing made, and two times in ten tries it as a named villager, whose name is then used up for good (the named villager's count is raised two times and lowered only one time when it is deleted, see row 401) | Ghidra `DoWallHuggerLookahead` (evaluate inlined: `Villager::Create(Pos, InfoList[0], 21, false)` then `ToBeDeleted(0)`), `Villager::Create` 0x74FBE0 (`GameRand(10)`, `SpecialVillager::Create` 0x71F1A0: count at +0x5C raised), `Villager::Villager` 0x74F950 (food, check-turn and 500 draws) | Names can run out sooner; the random stream moves on whenever a walker meets water, the land's edge or a fence | Kept | — | `PathfindingSystem::ProcessLookahead` / `TrialWalk`; bw1-decomp docs/openblack/terrain/pathfinding.md section 8 |
| 31 | Pathfinding (turned round into a pocket) | A walker turned round by its trial first jumps three steps on the way it was going, through whatever is there, and keeps only the across part of its step; the jump can put it in a pocket between circles with tiny gaps (a house's row of circles and a fence's), where at its own pace it goes round and round for good, while its stand-in, four times as fast, gets out with bigger steps, so later trials never turn it back | Ghidra `CircleHugStateInfoT::swap` 0x60A0B0 / `swapDirection` 0x60A030 (Pos + 3 x step, `step.Init(-x, -altitude, -z)`), the stand-in's `SetSpeed(speed << 2, 1)`. Seen in openblack with these rules on Land 1 (villager 123 between NORSE_ABODE_C at 1864.99, 2769.27 and the short Celtic fence at 1859.46, 2769.56, Land1.txt lines 46 and 1226: the house's two circles and the fence's three overlap in a closed ring round a small free patch; 3,000+ turns). Nothing in vanilla gets a walker out: the jump lands without any check (`Object::MoveMapObject` 0x636A40 / `ActualMoveMapObject` 0x638040 only re-file it), the walk has no turn budget or give-up (`Living::MoveToPos` 0x5EC270, `MobileWallHug::MoveTo`), the walk state's per-turn validate `Villager::WallhugValidate` is empty, and a walker is tried again only after leaving a circle or on a new walk (the completed set is cleared only in `MoveTo`'s exit, `SetupMobileMoveToPos` and `Reset`). Not confirmed in a vanilla run | A villager can stay stuck walking in a tiny loop until something else gives it a new task | Fixed (deviation #45) | User, 2026-10-12 ("Fix (deviation)") | `PathfindingSystem::TurnRound`, `wall_hug::TurnRound`; pathfinding.md section 8 |
| 32 | Reward chests (opened chest's fade and glow) | An opened chest is meant to fade out over its last 20 of 200 turns and to glow more and more blue as its lid lifts, but both values are written into the chest model's colours and then overwritten the same frame by the land lighting of the model, so neither ever shows. Corrected 2026-10-14 (branch reward-chest-light-colour): the light cone is still blue, because the opened model is drawn as a special light whose cone takes a constant blue (32, 61, 232) at full strength from the start; only the growing glow and the fade are lost. | `Reward::ProcessDrawing` 0x6E64C0: the alpha `(turns − 10)·255/20` into the object's colour +0x4C and the glow `0.3·place/playTime·(32, 61, 232)` into +0x50, then `fn_00801C90(&pos, &+0x4C, &+0x50)` at 0x6E670E rewrites both (also its other path `fn_007ACD90` → `fn_007A3EC0`). | 774 | Physics (physical shield, Khazar's Shield Challenge) | A contact between two bodies is as stiff and as damped as the softer of the two, and each body's stiffness and damping are its mass times its material's numbers. A physical shield's dome weighs 50000 times the cube of its scale, and its scale is 0.017 times its radius, so a small dome is very soft.
